- **Latest work (change request, out-of-phase — Phase 3)**: **DEV-32 (STU-27) — UGC video assembly — RE-RUN 2026-08-09 at the human's direction: "make FFmpeg as primary and video combiner as fallback". BUILT + completion-verified locally, COMMITTED `a92a6cc` on `main` + PUSHED to `origin/main` (2026-09-04, human-requested). Linear DEV-32 left In Progress; full description + AC written (the issue had none) + completion comment posted.** This reverses the 2026-07-10 `plan-phase-3.md` correction that made `video-combiner` primary, and restores what DEV-32's Linear title (`Video assembly (server-side FFmpeg: combine clips)`) said all along — `PROGRESS.md` had been flagging that title as "stale" since Phase 3. **Not a reordering: the FFmpeg path did not exist.** The original slice built only the `video-combiner` half and explicitly deferred the other ("FFmpeg fallback intentionally NOT built this slice" — `plan-phase-3.md` step 6), so this was a from-scratch build. New **`src/lib/ffmpeg.ts`**: download clips → `ffprobe` each → normalise (`scale` → `pad` → `setsar=1` → `fps`) → concat **filter** → re-encode H.264/AAC MP4 (`yuv420p`, `+faststart`); every impure edge injectable. `ugc-assembly.ts` became a primary/fallback orchestrator — FFmpeg first, `video-combiner` on **any** FFmpeg failure (missing binary, non-zero exit, download failure, timeout, or a failed master upload), with caller errors validated up front so they throw instead of burning $0.05. Result now carries `path` + `fallbackReason`. Binaries ship via `ffmpeg-static`/`ffprobe-static` (no Nix module — local dev exercises the same path as Replit); `ffmpeg-static` added to `pnpm-workspace.yaml → onlyBuiltDependencies` (its binary arrives in a postinstall script) and both added to `next.config.ts → serverExternalPackages` (bundling rewrites the `__dirname` they use to find their executable). **Per-video cost ≈$0.66 → ≈$0.61** (assembly is now $0.00). `typecheck ✅ · tests 414/414 ✅ (+29) · lint ✅ · build ✅`. **Completion-verified for real** via `scripts/qa-ffmpeg-assembly-probe.mjs` (uncommitted, per the qa-probe convention): deliberately mismatched fixtures — 1920×1080@24 **with audio** + 640×360@30 **silent** — produced one 1280×720@30 H.264/AAC master, duration 5.03s = 3+2. ⚠️ **Open risks, both human-gated:** (1) the **FFmpeg-on-Replit risk is deliberately re-opened** — the encode is CPU-bound inside a request on `deploymentTarget = "autoscale"`, unlike the submit-then-poll Muapi call it replaced; capped at 240s with fallback, but **never measured on real Replit autoscale with real ~15s clips**; (2) `video-combiner`'s live completion is **still** unverified (503 "at capacity" 60 submits / ~20h during the original build) — now less exposed, not proven. **One real behavioural difference:** `aspect_ratio: "auto"` means "inherit the first clip's ratio" to `video-combiner`; FFmpeg resolves it to 16:9 (1280×720), the orientation both upstream clips already default to.
- **Latest work (bugfix, out-of-slice)**: **DEV-69 — every `product_showcase` item failed generation (422) — FIXED (2026-08-06).** Reported as "the Instagram generation fails"; the platform was incidental — `product_showcase` is the only type routed to the product-photo pipeline. Root cause: `ai-product-shot` is **Image-to-Image** and requires `scene_description` + `image_url`, but `product-photo.ts` sent `prompt` (+ optional `image`) and the queue's `PhotoPipeline` had **no `sourceImageUrl` field at all** — so the required image could never be supplied. Business Profile `Product` has no image column, so this failed **100% of the time, every plan** (looked like a one-off because item failures are isolated and the plan still says "Your content is ready"). Fix: fall back to the social-graphic pipeline when a product has no photo (reusing the queue's existing "no products" fallback), correct the param names, fail loud without an image, and add `imageUrl`/`sourceImageUrl` as the seam for future product-image uploads. Also **surfaced the failure reason on the plan card** (new pure `src/lib/plan-errors.ts` — the stored error is raw provider output containing status codes, model names and the entire generated prompt, so it is translated, never interpolated). `typecheck ✅ · tests 340/340 ✅ (+9) · lint ✅ · build ✅`. **DEV-70 (framer-motion "invisible content") investigated → NOT A BUG, closed**: `requestAnimationFrame` never fires in the hidden Browser pane, so rAF-driven animations can't advance; CSS animations can, which masked it. No app defect; real users unaffected.

- **Current sub-task**: **STU-41 (DEV-43) — calendar status tracking — BUILT + browser-verified (2026-08-07), COMMITTED `73bdc45` + PUSHED to `origin/main`.** Two write-points, both best-effort, no schema change and no migration. **(1)** `CalendarService.markEntryGenerated` — a conditional owner-scoped UPDATE keyed on `(content_plan_id, plan_item_id)` whose `status = 'planned'` predicate stops a late generation write dragging a `scheduled`/`published` entry backwards — called from `generation-queue.ts` after an item completes, inside its own try/catch so calendar bookkeeping can never mark a successful generation `failed`. **(2)** A new `recordPublished` seam fired from `advanceJob` in `social-publishing.ts` (the single choke point both the scheduler's poll and `/social`'s read-poll pass through), which flips the Asset Kit via new `AssetKitService.markPublished` and the entries via new `CalendarService.markEntriesPublishedForKit`. Plus a "Published" badge on the Gallery card. Three gray areas were put to the human before any code and all three defaults were accepted: manual publishes flip the entry too; a *generation* failure leaves the entry `planned` (not `failed`); kit status is surfaced in the UI rather than data-only. **Prior sub-task**: **STU-40 (DEV-42) — scheduled publishing — DONE: committed `ef1bbb8` + pushed to `origin/main` (2026-08-07). - **Latest work (QA fix, 2026-08-11)**: **DEV-32 — assembly path is now visible in the logs. BUILT + verified end to end; NOT committed** (rides along with the still-uncommitted DEV-32 re-run). Closes the one finding QA run #2 could not tick off. The FFmpeg leg now writes its own `pipeline_logs` row — the thing it never did, because every other Agent Loop step is logged for free by `muapiService.generate` and FFmpeg makes no model call at all. **`model` carries the path**: a clean run writes `step=execute:ugc_assembly, model=ffmpeg, success=true, cost=0`, so "did the primary hold?" is a query. A **failed** attempt is logged too, with its reason, *before* the fallback runs — so a fallback reads as the pair `ffmpeg failed → video-combiner succeeded`, and the step's success rate in `getPipelineReliability` becomes a real measure of primary-path health instead of a constant 100%. **`durationMs` is recorded**, which means the Replit-autoscale encode time measures itself on every render — the open timing risk answers itself on deploy at no extra cost. Cost accounting is unchanged (the FFmpeg row is always 0; the fallback still logs its own $0.05 via Muapi). Logger is an injectable seam defaulting to `persistentPipelineLogger`, matching every sibling service; a throwing logger can never break a render (mirrors `MuapiService.log`). **No schema change, no migration, no UI.** **Deliberately scoped**: only the FFmpeg leg logs here (so the fallback isn't double-counted), and `path`/`fallbackReason` were **not** threaded up through `ugc-pipeline.ts` into the UGC Job row — the pipeline log answers the question without widening the step seam. `typecheck ✅ · tests 419/419 ✅ (+5) · lint ✅ · build ✅`. **Verified end to end against the real services** (`scripts/qa-ffmpeg-r2-probe.mjs`, uncommitted): real FFmpeg encode → real R2 → the emitted entry round-trips into the real Neon `pipeline_logs` table (`model=ffmpeg duration_ms=5852 success=true cost=0`; test row deleted after the read). - **Files modified this session (DEV-32 re-run, 2026-08-09)**:
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/ffmpeg.ts` (**new** — the FFmpeg assembly path the plan had deferred. Pure `buildProbeArgs`/`parseProbeOutput`/`buildConcatFilter`/`buildConcatArgs` + `FfmpegService.concatVideos`; `FfmpegUnavailableError` distinguishes "this environment cannot do FFmpeg" from "this encode failed". **Deliberately generic** — it knows nothing about talking heads or aspect-ratio enums, which is what stops it and `ugc-assembly.ts` importing each other in a cycle. **Two non-obvious calls, both load-bearing:** the cheap `-f concat` **demuxer** is unused because it needs bit-identical streams and the clips come from different models (it corrupts *silently* otherwise), and each clip is probed because `concat=a=1` fails outright on an input with no audio — i2v B-roll is silent while the talking head is not, so silent clips get an `anullsrc` track sized to their duration. Binary resolution uses dynamic `import()`, **not `require()`** — `require` is undefined under real ESM, where it would throw, get swallowed, and make FFmpeg look permanently unavailable, silently downgrading every render to the fallback. That bug was hit and fixed during this session.)
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/ffmpeg.test.ts` (**new** — +20 tests: probe argv + parsing incl. the silent/no-duration fallbacks, filter normalisation, silent-input index arithmetic across multiple silent clips, argv encode flags, probe/input count mismatch, and the service's download→probe→run→read flow incl. cleanup on both the success and failure paths)
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/ugc-assembly.ts` (amended — now a primary/fallback orchestrator. **New** `ASPECT_DIMENSIONS` (every dimension even — `yuv420p` rejects odd) + `resolveConcatTarget`, `AssemblyPath`, `FFMPEG_ASSEMBLY_LABEL`, `AssemblyFfmpeg`/`UploadMaster` seams, `path` + `fallbackReason` on the result, `userId` on the request. The upload sits **inside** the try on purpose: a master the reframe step cannot fetch is a failed path whatever the cause. The original `video-combiner` code moved intact into `assembleWithCombiner`.)
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/ugc-assembly.test.ts` (amended — +9 net; combiner tests now have to force an FFmpeg failure to reach the fallback. New coverage: FFmpeg primary never touches Muapi, $0 cost, concrete pixels per ratio, userId → upload, fallback on encode failure / unavailable binary / failed upload, both-paths-fail propagation, and that a caller error is **not** a reason to fall back)
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/ugc-pipeline.ts` (amended — `AssemblyStep` gained `userId` (scopes the master's R2 key) + threaded from `job.userId` at the call site and through `defaultAssembly`)
  - `artifacts/web/src/lib/model-router.ts` (amended — the `video_assemble` comment claimed `video-combiner` was primary; now records it as fallback-only, and that a `video_assemble` row in the pipeline logs is a signal worth reading rather than routine traffic)

Onboarding auto-embedding, content planner, retrieval, and text generation are no longer key-blocked at runtime. - **✅ Muapi free-tier shim RESTORED (2026-07-16, reverted a bad change):** a prior working-tree change had DELETED `muapi-availability.ts` / `resolveAvailableModel` and the `resolveModel` seams, on the false claim the key was "upgraded and all models verified." **Live re-testing proved that false** — only `nano-banana-2` completes; `sdxl-image` and `google-imagen4-fast` return `failed`/`Model not found`, `flux-schnell` 404s, and `ai-product-shot` is Image-to-Image (422 without an input image). This made every content-plan item show **failed**. Fix: restored the shim + call sites + routing table to committed HEAD (`git checkout HEAD -- …`). Verified end-to-end: `ProductPhotoService` + `SocialGraphicService` both complete live via `nano-banana-2` → real CDN URLs. Shim must stay until slugs are individually live-verified. See `.agents/memory/muapi-api-contract.md`.

## Concepts Introduced (cumulative)

- DEV-3: Next.js framework, TypeScript strict mode, App Router, Tailwind CSS, shadcn/ui component library
- DEV-1: Schema-as-code (Drizzle ORM type-safe table definitions), fail-fast env validation (Zod), Neon serverless PostgreSQL
- DEV-2: Auth middleware (edge-level route protection), catch-all auth routes (Clerk sub-step routing)
- DEV-4: Webhook signature verification (svix), idempotent writes (duplicate-safe DB operations), middleware bypass (whitelist pattern for server-to-server routes)
- DEV-5: Progressive Enhancement (Server Components + Client Components), SEO as Infrastructure (metadata exports, OG images, structured data), Component Locality (splitting pages into focused sections)
- DEV-6: Route Group Isolation (dashboard-only layouts), Dynamic Rendering for Auth-Dependent Pages, CSS-Only Transitions for Collapsible UI
- DEV-7: Fail-fast configuration for third-party services (Muapi, OpenAI, R2), building against a hardcoded fixture ahead of real onboarding/DB data
- DEV-8: External API cost metering (parsing per-call cost from response headers), server-side asset persistence (download-then-upload to object storage instead of trusting a third-party URL to stay alive), path-based service routing (why two backend services can't claim the same URL prefix)
- DEV-13: Structured output (constraining an LLM to return a validated shape instead of parsing free text), pipeline chaining (composing independent AI calls into one user-facing flow), unit economics (per-call cost tracking across multiple paid services)
- DEV-9: Data ownership at the database level (foreign keys + auth-derived queries instead of client-supplied IDs), validation at the edge (rejecting malformed input before it reaches business logic/DB)
- DEV-10: Single source of truth for multi-step forms (parent-owned state surviving navigation), two-layer validation (client UX checks + server safety checks), graceful conflict recovery (409 → route the user forward instead of erroring)
- DEV-11: Content-based file validation (magic bytes over client-claimed MIME), SVG-as-active-content risk (sanitize + Content-Disposition: attachment), functional state updates (merging async results against latest state, not captured state), client-side pixel analysis (canvas getImageData color quantization)
- DEV-12: Non-destructive defaults (auto-filling a form only where the user hasn't already typed something, so a preset never clobbers real input), exhaustiveness checking (TypeScript's `Record<enum, T>` forcing every enum value to have a corresponding data entry, catching an incomplete data file at compile time instead of at runtime), data provenance (recording which preset seeded a record so later features can build on the original choice)
- DEV-59: Just-in-time provisioning (creating a dependent record on first authenticated use instead of trusting an async webhook to have run), webhook-as-primary/JIT-as-fallback sync (eventual consistency between an external auth provider and the local DB), race-safe idempotent insert (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` + re-select against a unique key)
- DEV-14: Redirect-as-control-flow (a server page deciding whether to render or reroute before any HTML is sent), server-side data fetching (querying the DB in the component instead of a client fetch + loading state), closing the onboarding loop (reflecting a user's own entered data back to them)
- DEV-15: Retry with exponential backoff (surviving transient third-party failures — network/429/5xx — by waiting progressively longer instead of failing the user on the first hiccup), dependency injection for testability (swapping fetch/sleep/clock/key from outside so retry/backoff/timeout paths test instantly and offline), typed domain errors (a `MuapiError` carrying HTTP status + a `retryable` flag so callers branch on failure kind), environment-agnostic test harness (Node's built-in runner + native TS type-stripping when the toolchain's usual runner can't run in the sandbox)
- DEV-17: Vector embeddings + semantic memory (representing brand text as 1536-dim vectors so "most relevant to this generation" becomes a distance query instead of stuffing the whole profile into every prompt), pgvector as a first-class column type (storing/indexing vectors in Postgres with an HNSW cosine index for approximate-nearest-neighbour search), replace-on-sync idempotency (re-deriving a user's profile embeddings on every edit instead of accumulating stale chunks, scoped by `kind` so later generation embeddings survive), fault isolation for secondary effects (a non-fatal auto-embedding step that can never fail the primary profile save)
- DEV-16: Retrieval-augmented generation (fetching only the top-k relevant brand chunks per request instead of injecting the whole profile — cost + relevance scale with data), cosine similarity ranking (ordering stored vectors by angular closeness to a query vector so semantically related text surfaces even with no shared keywords), service composition via reuse (retrieval's query embedder reuses the KB's `openAIEmbedder` so the embedding model + cost stay single-sourced)
- DEV-18: Configuration over hard-coding (model choices live in one data table, not scattered through pipeline code, so swapping a model or tier is a one-line change), compile-time exhaustiveness (`Record<AssetType, …>` forces a route for every asset type — a missing one fails to build instead of at generation time), infrastructure hiding (model slugs never reach user-facing code — "invisible to users", credits only), graceful capability fallback (premium quality degrades to the standard model when no premium exists rather than erroring)
- DEV-20: Service composition / orchestration (the planner does little itself — it wires retrieval + industry strategy + LLM together, keeping each part independently testable), trust-but-verify LLM output (a schema constrains shape but not meaning, so structured output is still validated/normalized — item cap, type filter, platform snap — before use), grounding to fight generic output (feeding the model business-specific inputs — industry rhythm, RAG-retrieved brand facts, real upcoming holidays — so proposals are about *this* business and *this* week), forward-compatible seams (an unused `performanceInsights` arg lets DEV-25 wire the feedback loop in without a signature change)
- DEV-19: Pipeline orchestration with optional stages (a multi-step generation flow where bg-removal/reframe run only when they apply, with per-step cost aggregation), isolating a temporary workaround (a single named `resolveAvailableModel` boundary + TODO contains the free-tier model shim so the rest of the code stays written against the real Model Router — a one-line deletion later), passthrough observability (passing step labels into the Muapi service so each generation self-logs without the pipeline needing its own logger)
- DEV-21: DRY for a shared invariant (extracting the free-tier model shim to one module the moment a second pipeline needed it — because "which model this key can call" is a single decision that must stay consistent, unlike incidental look-alike code), data-encoded configuration (format→aspect-ratio as a lookup table, not scattered conditionals, so a new format is a one-line change), knowing when NOT to over-share (photo vs graphic keep separate focused services — they share the real machinery but differ for different reasons-to-change)
- DEV-22: One conditioning pattern, many outputs (captions/ad copy/plans/photos/graphics all follow retrieve → grounded prompt → model → clean up, so brand consistency is structural and improving retrieval improves everything), sanitizing at the boundary (hashtags normalized once in the producing service — strip `#`, dedupe, cap — so every consumer receives one canonical form), distinguishing env failures from code failures (a dead API key verified independently against the provider, so the slice isn't blocked on a phantom bug)
- DEV-23: Owning your outputs (copying generated media into app-controlled storage at creation time, because vendor URLs make no liveness promise — dead-link galleries destroy trust), ordering side effects by what must not fail (critical path download→store→save completes before the optional embed-back runs, so a soft failure can never lose paid work), closing the learning loop (each completed generation is embedded back into the Brand Knowledge Base, so future plans/captions retrieve what was already made)
- DEV-24: Status as a state machine (plan + per-item states persisted in the DB so any client renders exact progress from stored truth — survives refresh/crash), optimistic UI with reconciliation (edits/approve update the screen instantly, sync to the server, roll back on rejection; polling reconciles during generation), batch failure isolation (Promise.allSettled + per-item status so one failed generation never sinks the plan, and retry re-runs only what failed), inline-vs-background job trade-off (queue runs inline in the approve request — fine for free-tier/mock latency, flagged as tech debt for real model latency)
- DEV-26: Lookahead pagination (fetch page-size+1 rows to derive `hasMore` without a second count query on the hot path), optimistic interactions with rollback (thumbs tap updates instantly, reverts on server rejection), idempotent per-user rating (unique (user, kit) + `onConflictDoUpdate` so a re-tap updates one row rather than accumulating), feedback as a first-class learning signal (ratings stored to later bias the planner via the Performance Feedback Loop)
- DEV-25: Self-evaluation control loop (the agent scores its own plans and auto-regenerates below a threshold — bounded retries — so weak output never ships), the feedback loop closes (aggregated thumbs up/down become a planner prompt hint, so the product compounds in quality with use), observability as data (every pipeline step emits a structured `pipeline_logs` row → reliability/cost are a query, not a guess), safe circular type-imports (muapi ↔ pipeline-log cycle resolved because the back-reference is type-only and erased at runtime)
- STU-C3 (DEV-62): Trust the bytes, not the label (validate uploads by sniffing magic bytes server-side because the client-supplied MIME type is attacker-controlled — same lesson as DEV-11's logo route, now generalized to a photo library), thin adapter over a testable core (Server Actions stay a bare auth + FormData shell while all validation/storage lives in an injectable-seam service that unit-tests with no network/DB), authorization at the query (ownership-scoped delete matches `id` AND `userId` so a guessed id can't touch another user's row), source material vs output (the Media Library — a user's own uploads — is a distinct concept from the Gallery of generated Asset Kits, and is what unblocks image-to-image pipelines)
- STU-25 (DEV-29): Read the schema before trusting the name (the plan's `creatify-lipsync` is a lip-sync model, but "lip-sync" splits into video-driven [re-sync an existing clip] vs image-driven [animate a portrait] — only the live `input_schema` reveals which, and the named model was the wrong half), the data model constrains the model choice (a portrait-only presenter library forces an `image_url`+`audio_url` model regardless of price, so the cheapest option that fits the data beats the cheapest option overall), completion-verify at the seam that will run in prod (probe drove the real `MuapiService` with a real portrait + a real generated voiceover, proving the exact VO→lip-sync handoff, not a hypothetical), dynamic pricing is a range not a number (record real `X-MuAPI-Cost-USD` per step + keep a per-video ceiling alert, because a one-sample probe underestimates a longer script)
- STU-C7 (DEV-64): Foundation before feature (build the presenter catalogue Phase 3 hard-depends on ahead of the pipeline that consumes it, so the dependency is ready and not discovered mid-build), MVP scope discipline (ship a static typed roster + filter UI to fully meet the AC, deferring the DB table and — honestly flagged — the real portrait *bitmaps*, rather than gold-plating), placeholder with a paper trail (a reproducible seed script + a memory carryover flag so a stand-in asset can never be mistaken for the real thing or silently ship to Phase 3's lip-sync), data-shape stability across a swap (`imageUrl` is the single seam so real R2 portraits drop in later without touching the roster, its consumers, or its tests), convention over the plan's letter (a typed TS-data module beats the plan's literal `data/presenters.json` — types + a test runner that can actually import it — the same call STU-C6/DEV-20 made)

Zero-dependency plain Node; POSTs with the `CRON_SECRET` bearer, logs the scheduler summary, and **exits non-zero on any non-2xx** so a mismatched secret shows up as a failed run rather than silence.

Confirmed `ffprobe-static@3.1.0` ships prebuilt `linux/` binaries and needs no postinstall approval, unlike `ffmpeg-static` (already in `onlyBuiltDependencies`).

Deleted both JSON files and wrote a single **`.railway/railway.ts`** declaring both services (`railway config init` scaffolded it; the file is hand-written because `railway config migrate` only handles one service and names it after the directory). Added `railway@3.11.0` as a **root devDependency** — `railway config plan` refuses to run without the SDK.
- **`railway config plan` → `2 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy`.** The authoring file is validated against a real project, not just eyeballed.

Prints key names only, never values, and skips the Railway-managed `PORT`/`NODE_ENV`. Deliberately a script **the human runs**, not something the agent executes.

**Decisions made (with rationale)**
- **Root Directory = repo root for both services, not `artifacts/web`.** This is a shared pnpm monorepo; the `catalog:` versions and the single lockfile only resolve from the root. Documented prominently because it is the most likely first-deploy failure.
- **No `output: "standalone"`.** Standalone's file tracing does not reliably carry the `ffmpeg-static` / `ffprobe-static` native binaries that `next.config.ts` deliberately keeps external. Bigger image, working render path.
- **Cron over the public domain, not Railway's private network.** The private network is IPv6-only and `next start --hostname 0.0.0.0` binds IPv4. Noted in `DEPLOYMENT.md` alongside the "keep `CRON_SECRET` strong" consequence.
- **Cron as a second service rather than an in-process scheduler** — keeps DEV-42's existing HTTP contract untouched.

- **No application code changed.** Two risks were found and flagged rather than fixed, per scope discipline: (1) `/api/social/connect` builds its OAuth `redirect_uri` from `new URL(req.url).origin`, which behind a TLS-terminating proxy may yield `http://` and break the round-trip — needs either a live check on first connect or an `x-forwarded-proto` fix; (2) there is still no health endpoint, so the healthcheck points at `/`.
- **Linear not updated** — no Linear MCP in this session. This is infra with no slice issue; open a Linear issue for the Railway migration if it should be tracked.

**Blockers**: ~~`main` has uncommitted work~~ — **CLEARED 2026-09-04 at the human's request** ("commit + push the working tree"). Three commits, each self-consistent for a `--frozen-lockfile` install: `a92a6cc` (DEV-32 FFmpeg promotion + both `package.json`s + `pnpm-workspace.yaml` + `pnpm-lock.yaml` + `plan-phase-3.md` + `CONTEXT.md`), `2e91636` (Railway config), and this `PROGRESS.md` sync. Fast-forward pushed to `origin/main`. **The lockfile could not be split** — pnpm keeps one for the workspace, and it carries both `ffmpeg-static` and the `railway` devDependency, so `a92a6cc` deliberately holds the root `package.json` change belonging to `2e91636`; splitting it would have left one commit with manifest/lockfile drift. Noted in the commit body. Re-verified before committing: `typecheck ✅ · tests 419/419 ✅ · lint ✅` (build verified earlier this session).

**Correction to the 2026-08-27 half of this entry**: it claimed a GitHub-sourced build would "fail the install outright" on lockfile/manifest drift. That was wrong — `git show HEAD:` on both files showed `HEAD`'s `package.json` and lockfile were self-consistent (neither mentioned `ffmpeg-static`), so `origin/main` **would** have built. The real consequence was quieter and worse: it would have shipped without the FFmpeg primary path, silently falling back to the paid `video-combiner` on every render.

**Clerk production switch (2026-09-04, human-directed — reverses the same day's earlier decision)**: the deploy is now backed by a Clerk **production** instance, not the dev/test keys. This surfaced a prerequisite that was not obvious: **a Clerk production instance cannot be issued against a `*.up.railway.app` host.** Clerk validates production by DNS records (CNAMEs for `clerk`/`accounts` + DKIM mail records) on a domain the human controls, and CNAMEs cannot be added to `railway.app` — so **a custom domain is now a hard prerequisite for the deploy**, not a nice-to-have. Confirmed against Clerk's docs, including the `Production Keys are only allowed for domain "…"` failure mode and the Cloudflare "DNS-only / grey cloud" gotcha (a proxied record fails Clerk's DNS check).

Consequences worked through and documented in `DEPLOYMENT.md` (§2 prerequisite, §3 env matrix, new §5 *Domain + Clerk production instance*, §6 risks): (1) production keys are **domain-locked** and error out on localhost, so they cannot replace `.env.local` — they live in a separate gitignored `artifacts/web/.env.production.local`, templated by the new committed `deploy/railway/env.production.example`; (2) `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is **inlined at build time**, so switching it needs `railway redeploy`, not a restart — a `curl | grep pk_` check on the served page catches a stale bundle; (3) the production instance needs its **own webhook endpoint + signing secret** (different from dev) and its **own OAuth provider credentials** — Clerk's dev-mode shared OAuth apps do not carry over.

`deploy/railway/sync-env.sh` was reworked to enforce this rather than just document it: it takes `--file`/`--service`, skips the two `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_SIGN_*_URL` values now declared in `.railway/railway.ts`, and **hard-refuses to push a `pk_test_`/`sk_test_` key** (escape hatch `--allow-test-keys`). Verified by running it against the real `.env.local`, which does hold a `pk_test_` key — it refused, as intended.

**Deliberate stopping point**: `railway config apply` was **not** run. It would create both services and start a build with no secrets set, which would crash-loop on `src/env.ts` validation. (The second reason — that it would build an `origin/main` predating the FFmpeg work — was cleared by the 2026-09-04 push.) The remaining steps — `config apply`, the cron-schedule CLI command, `sync-env.sh --apply` — are sequenced in `DEPLOYMENT.md § 4`, and are now **blocked on the human supplying a custom domain** (see the Clerk note above) before the first shippable build.

**Tech debt observed**: `.replit`, `.replitignore` and `next.config.ts`'s `allowedDevOrigins` are Replit-only leftovers; harmless in production, not worth removing until Replit is retired. The `allowBuilds:` placeholder in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is dead config.

### 2026-08-11 — QA run #2 (fresh session) + logging fix: DEV-32 (STU-27) assembly checklist — 5/6 pass, 2 corrections to the prior run

**What completed**
- Static feedback loop: `typecheck ✅ · tests 414/414 ✅ · lint ✅ · build ✅` (no co-running dev server — full `.next` build).
- Re-ran the existing `scripts/qa-ffmpeg-checklist.mjs` harness → 10/10 sub-checks pass (encode, geometry, duration drift 0.00s, single continuous audio track, presenter audio present at −24.1 dB, silent B-roll leg at −91.0 dB, and three distinct fallback modes: broken binary / no binary / undownloadable clip).
- **New `scripts/qa-ffmpeg-r2-probe.mjs`** (uncommitted, qa-probe convention) — closes the storage checklist item for real: FFmpeg-encode → real R2 `PutObject` → fetch the public URL back → `ffprobe` the returned bytes.
- **New `scripts/qa-ffmpeg-timing-probe.mjs`** (uncommitted) — runs the exact production argv from `buildConcatArgs` at 1 / 2 / all threads to bound the FFmpeg-on-Replit risk against CPU count.
- Posted QA run #2 as a comment on Linear DEV-32. Left **In Progress** (unchanged — the work is still uncommitted and two items are still open).

**Two corrections to the 2026-08-09 QA comment**
1. **The storage item's pass was circular.** It was answered against a stub `upload` seam that hand-wrote the key string, so it only proved the test agreed with itself. Re-tested against the real bucket: key `ugc-assembly/qa_e720ef9b/…` correct, HTTP 200 `video/mp4` 0.49 MB, round-tripped bytes probe as h264 1280×720 + aac 20.00s, `path: "ffmpeg"`, `cost: 0`, fallback never contacted. **One QA object was left in R2 — delete when convenient.**
2. **"Local is 5.6s … on an M-series Mac" is wrong.** The host is a 2016 **Intel Core i5-6360U @ 2.0 GHz** (2 cores / 4 threads). That inverts the conclusion the prior run drew: the local number is a *pessimistic* proxy for a Replit autoscale vCPU, not an optimistic one.

**Timing, now bounded** (20s master = 15s presenter w/ audio + 5s silent B-roll): 1 thread **4.4s**, 2 threads **4.2s**, 4 threads **4.5s** — ≈1.8% of the 240s cap, and it barely scales with cores, so a 1-vCPU box is not meaningfully worse. Pathological content (mandelbrot zoom + pure random noise) → **14.3s single-threaded, 6% of cap**. Still not measured on Replit and excludes CDN download of the real clips, so the item is **bounded, not closed** — but "cannot responsibly estimate" no longer holds.

**Finding raised, then fixed in the same session at the human's go-ahead**: "confirm from the logs which path ran" was impossible, and marginally worse than last reported — `defaultAssembly` in `ugc-pipeline.ts` destructures only `{ videoUrl, cost }`, dropping `path`/`fallbackReason`, and cost is only accumulated into `totalCost`, so the `$0.00 vs $0.05` inference wasn't available either. **Hit the consequence live**: a malformed R2 endpoint made the upload throw `Invalid URL`, the run completed "successfully" via the paid fallback with a normal-looking video URL, and nothing surfaced it — a misconfigured R2 in production would have silently downgraded every render to the $0.05 path. Surfaced it as a scope call rather than fixing it unasked; the human said apply it.

**The fix (second half of the session)**: the FFmpeg leg writes its own `pipeline_logs` row with `model` as the path discriminator, the failure reason on a failed attempt (logged *before* the fallback runs), and `durationMs` — which makes the open Replit timing risk self-measuring on the first real render. Injectable `logger` seam defaulting to `persistentPipelineLogger`; throwing loggers can't break a render. No schema change. Only the FFmpeg leg logs, so the fallback isn't double-counted; `path` was deliberately **not** threaded up into the UGC Job row (the pipeline log answers it without widening the step seam). `typecheck ✅ · tests 419/419 ✅ (+5) · lint ✅ · build ✅`, plus a real FFmpeg → real R2 → real Neon round-trip of the emitted entry.

**Decision made (with rationale)**: log the FFmpeg leg only, not both branches. Writing our own row for the fallback too would duplicate the row Muapi already writes under the same step and double-count its $0.05. As built, a fallback reads as the pair `ffmpeg failed → video-combiner succeeded`, which also turns the step's success rate in `getPipelineReliability` into a real measure of primary-path health rather than a constant 100%.

**What deferred (and why)**
- **One real UGC render end to end** — needs the Replit deployment, real presenter portraits (still placeholder SVGs), and ~$0.51 of real spend. **This is now the only open checklist item.**
- **Still not committed** — the human's go-ahead covers the QA and the fix, not the checkpoint.
- **Two QA video objects left in R2** (`ugc-assembly/qa_e720ef9b/…`, `ugc-assembly/qa_5c39b687/…`) — flagged for the human rather than deleted.

**Tech debt observed**: none new. (The `.env.local` values are written as `KEY= value` with a leading space — harmless for dotenv, which trims, but it cost time in a hand-rolled probe parser.)

### 2026-08-09 — Phase 3 (out-of-phase change request): DEV-32 (STU-27) assembly — FFmpeg promoted to PRIMARY, video-combiner demoted to fallback — BUILT + completion-verified, UNCOMMITTED

**Phase**: 3 — UGC Video Pipeline (`plan-phase-3.md`), reached out of order. The active phase is still Phase 5. **Slice**: DEV-32 / STU-27, re-run. Interactive mode, human present. Trigger: "Re run DEV-32 and make FFmpeg as primary and video combiner as fallback."

**Handled as a change request** per `AGENTS.md → Handling change requests`, not as a fresh slice: detected the type (rework of an In-Progress slice — so editable in place, not requiring a new slice), asked the two material questions before writing any code, then propagated to every affected location.

**Two clarifying questions asked, both answered with the recommended default:**
1. *How does the FFmpeg binary reach the runtime?* → **`ffmpeg-static` npm dep** (over a `.replit` Nix module, which would exist only on Replit and mean the primary path is never exercised locally; and over WASM, too slow for in-request encoding).
2. *When does it fall back?* → **any FFmpeg failure** (over environment-failures-only, and over deleting the combiner entirely).

**What completed**
- **`src/lib/ffmpeg.ts` (new)** — the FFmpeg path. The plan had explicitly deferred it ("FFmpeg fallback intentionally NOT built this slice"), so despite the word "re-run" this was a from-scratch build; the pre-existing `video-combiner` code needed only to be moved into a fallback branch.
- **`ugc-assembly.ts`** — primary/fallback orchestrator; result carries `path` + `fallbackReason`.
- Deps, `pnpm-workspace.yaml → onlyBuiltDependencies`, `next.config.ts → serverExternalPackages`, `ugc-pipeline.ts` (`userId` on the assembly seam), `model-router.ts` comment.
- Docs: `plan-phase-3.md` (header note, slice line, cost table, step 6, Risks), `CONTEXT.md` (**new** term **Assembly Path**), `PROGRESS.md`.
- Linear DEV-32: **wrote the description + acceptance criteria from scratch** (the issue had none at all) + posted the completion comment. Left **In Progress** — this team has no Needs Review status, and the live QA is outstanding.

**Decisions made (with rationale)**
- **Concat filter, not the `-f concat` demuxer.** The demuxer requires bit-identical stream parameters and the clips come from *different* models (`infinitetalk-image-to-video` vs `kling-v2.1-standard-i2v`). It corrupts **silently** on mismatch. Normalising (`scale` → `pad` → `setsar=1` → `fps`) and re-encoding is slower but cannot produce a subtly broken master.
- **Probe every clip before building the graph.** `concat=a=1` fails outright on an input with no audio stream. i2v B-roll is normally silent while the talking head is not — so this is the *guaranteed* shape of the real pipeline, not an edge case. Silent clips get an `anullsrc` track sized to their duration.
- **Fallback fires on any FFmpeg failure incl. a failed master upload**, but **caller errors are validated up front and throw.** A malformed request fails identically on both paths, so paying $0.05 to rediscover it is waste; an environment/encode failure is worth $0.05 to recover from.
- **`auto` resolves to 16:9 on the FFmpeg path** — a genuine behavioural difference from `video-combiner`, which inherits the first clip's ratio. Documented in three places rather than hidden; 16:9 is what both upstream clips already default to.
- **`userId` added to the `AssemblyStep` seam.** Required by the change, not scope creep: FFmpeg produces bytes, not a URL, and the bytes need a per-user R2 home the reframe step can fetch.

**Bug found and fixed mid-session**: binary resolution first used bare `require()`, which is **undefined under real ESM** — it threw, got swallowed by the `catch`, and made FFmpeg look permanently unavailable, silently downgrading every render to the paid fallback with no error anywhere. Caught only because the real-ffmpeg probe failed; the 20 unit tests all passed against it, since they inject the binary path. Switched to dynamic `import()`, which works in both ESM and the transpiled CJS server bundle.

**Test/lint/build**: `typecheck ✅ · tests 414/414 ✅ (+29) · lint ✅ · build ✅`. **Completion-verified for real** (`scripts/qa-ffmpeg-assembly-probe.mjs`, uncommitted): 1920×1080@24 **with audio** + 640×360@30 **silent** → one 1280×720@30 H.264/AAC master, 5.03s = 3+2.

**What deferred (and why)**
- **Not committed.** Interactive mode; the human's instruction covered the work, not the checkpoint. Awaiting go-ahead.
- **Real Replit autoscale timing of the encode** — the re-opened FFmpeg-on-Replit risk. Cannot be measured from a laptop; on the DEV-32 QA checklist.
- **A real UGC render through the FFmpeg path** — needs the deployment plus the Phase-3 carryovers (placeholder presenter SVGs).
- **`video-combiner` completion still unverified** — untouched by this change; it is now less exposed (only reached after FFmpeg fails), not proven.

**Tech debt observed**
- `CONTEXT.md → UGC Pipeline` still says "~$0.91" per video; `plan-phase-3.md` now says ≈$0.61. That drift **predates this session** (the plan already said ≈$0.66) — left alone per the no-drive-by-refactors rule.
- Linear titles across Phase 3 remain stale vs the plan (DEV-28 `mmaudio`, DEV-31 `luma-flash-reframe`). DEV-32's title is the one that just *stopped* being stale. Worth a sweep.
- `ffprobe-static` resolves the `darwin/x64` binary on this arm64 Mac (runs under Rosetta, verified working). Linux/Replit will pick its own; no action, noted so a future arm64-only environment isn't a surprise.

### 2026-08-07 — Phase 5: DEV-43 (STU-41) calendar status tracking — BUILT + browser-verified + COMMITTED `73bdc45` + PUSHED

**Phase**: 5 — Content Calendar + Scheduling (`plan-phase-5.md`). **Slice**: DEV-43 / STU-41, the last Phase-5 slice, unblocked by DEV-42. Interactive mode, human present, explicit go-ahead ("go ahead and start DEV-43").

**Completed**: The status flow now advances itself end to end. **(1)** `planned` → `generated`: `CalendarService.markEntryGenerated` attaches the freshly generated Asset Kit and advances the entry, called from `generation-queue.ts` after an item completes. **(2)** → `published`: a new `recordPublished` seam on `advanceJob` in `social-publishing.ts` flips the Asset Kit (`AssetKitService.markPublished`, new) and every entry carrying it (`CalendarService.markEntriesPublishedForKit`, new). **(3)** A "Published" badge on the Gallery card. The Linear issue had **no description or acceptance criteria** — wrote the full spec (goal, scope, 9 GIVEN/WHEN/THEN ACs, out-of-scope) before starting, same as DEV-42.

**Finding that reframed the slice**: this was not bookkeeping polish. Entries are created `planned` with a null `asset_kit_id` at plan-approval time, and **nothing ever updated them** — generation wrote the kit id onto the *Content Plan Item* only. DEV-42's opt-in requires `generated` **and** a kit, so **scheduled publishing was unreachable through the normal product flow**. Separately, `asset_kits.status = 'published'` had been declared in the schema since DEV-23 and written by nothing. Both are now closed.

**Decisions (with rationale)** — all three gray areas went to the human before any code, all three defaults accepted:
- **A manual publish flips the Calendar Entry too**, not just the scheduler's. Hooking at `advanceJob` — the one point both the cron's poll and `/social`'s read-poll pass through — covers both paths with a single write; hooking anywhere else means writing it twice and watching it drift. Overlaps DEV-42's own `published` patch, which is deliberate and idempotent.
- **A generation failure leaves the entry `planned`, not `failed`.** `failed` already means *publish* failed, and its retry path (re-scheduling) assumes an Asset Kit exists. A generation failure has none, so overloading `failed` would offer a retry that cannot work. The item's own failure + Retry already surface on `/plan`.
- **Kit status is surfaced in the Gallery**, not left as data-only — otherwise half the slice is invisible and only verifiable in the DB.
- **Both writes are best-effort, and the placement is the point.** The generation hook sits in its *own* try/catch *inside* `processItem`'s outer try — one level out and a calendar hiccup would mark a successful, paid-for generation `failed`. The publish hook runs after the job row is already persisted, so a bookkeeping failure can never cost the user the record of a real post.
- **`markEntryGenerated` resolves `null` on no-match instead of throwing** (unlike `setEntrySchedule`, which throws). No-match is an ordinary outcome on a background path — the plan may have no entries, or the entry may be further along.
- **The default recorder uses `Promise.allSettled`, not `await`-in-sequence**, so a failure flipping the kit doesn't silently skip the calendar write (it still rethrows, to be caught and logged upstream).

**Verified**: `pnpm typecheck` ✅, `pnpm test` ✅ (385 tests, +16 this slice, all green), `pnpm lint` ✅, `pnpm build` ✅. Badge verified in-browser via a throwaway fixture at `/qa-gallery-card` (since deleted): published card shows the badge, `ready` card doesn't, and on hover the badge fades so the type badge takes the corner cleanly. No console errors. Every new conditional-update rule is covered by a test against injected seams.

**Deferred (not silently dropped)**:
- **No end-to-end run against real data.** Both write-points are unit-verified through seams, but nothing has been exercised against Neon — that needs a plan approval + a real publish, which is the same human-gated QA DEV-42 is already waiting on. It is on the QA checklist.
- **The Gallery lightbox does not show published state**, only the card. Out of scope as specified.
- **No un-publish / reconciliation** if a post is deleted on the platform side — a kit stays `published` forever.

**Tech debt observed**:
- **`published` is terminal with no way back.** Nothing can un-publish an entry or kit, including after a `failed` retry that later succeeds against a deleted post. Fine for now; worth a real state machine if publishing grows.
- **`asset_kits.status` is now written from two modules** (`asset-kit.ts` at assembly, `social-publishing.ts` via the recorder). Still one *owner* (`AssetKitService`), but worth watching that the kit lifecycle doesn't sprout more writers.
- **Still no automated coverage for route handlers or React components** anywhere in the repo (carried over from DEV-42) — the badge is verified by eye only, and there is no component-test setup to add a smoke test to without introducing one.
- `PROGRESS.md` "Current status" remains one enormous line; splitting it per phase is still overdue.
- **`next build` and `next dev` share one `.next` directory, and so do two co-running dev servers.** Hit for real this session: running `pnpm build` while another chat's dev server had been live since 15:58 overwrote its compiled output, and starting a second dev server overwrote it again — the older server then threw `__webpack_modules__[moduleId] is not a function` + `ChunkLoadError` on `app/(dashboard)/layout.js`, because its in-memory module graph pointed at files that had been replaced twice. Recovered by killing both servers (the wrapper's `next-server` child survived its parent and needed a second kill), deleting the 902 MB `.next`, and restarting one server on 5050. **DEV-39 hit the same trap** and worked around it with a temporary env-gated `distDir` override that was then reverted — so this is now the second occurrence. The durable fix is a distinct `distDir` per runner (build vs dev, and per dev server) rather than remembering not to build while a server is up. Not fixed in this slice: it is a tooling change, unrelated to DEV-43's diff.

**Committed `73bdc45` + pushed to `origin/main`** later the same session at the human's explicit request ("commit and push DEV-43"); fast-forward `02c0fb9..73bdc45`. Re-ran the full feedback loop immediately before committing — `typecheck ✅ · tests 385/385 ✅ · lint ✅`. The `scripts/*.mjs` probes and the untracked `.claude/` runner configs were deliberately left out of the commit, per the existing convention. Linear DEV-43 → In Progress with description + ACs + approach note + completion comment; left In Progress because this team has no Needs Review status and live QA is outstanding.

### 2026-08-07 — Phase 5: DEV-42 (STU-40) scheduled publishing — BUILT + live-probed

**Phase**: 5 — Content Calendar + Scheduling (`plan-phase-5.md`). **Slice**: DEV-42 / STU-40, unblocked by DEV-41 (Done). Interactive mode, human present, explicit go-ahead ("go ahead, start DEV-42").

**Completed**: Scheduled publishing end to end. New **Publish Scheduler** (`src/lib/publish-scheduler.ts`): one run advances every in-flight Calendar Entry by polling its Publish Job (`completed` → entry `published`, `failed` → entry `failed`), then claims and submits every entry now due. New **`POST`/`GET /api/cron/publish`** behind an `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` header (unset secret → 503, fail closed), bypassed in `middleware.ts` like the Clerk webhook. New **auto-publish opt-in** in the calendar day panel + `CalendarService.setEntrySchedule` + a `scheduled` branch on `PATCH /api/calendar`. `calendar_entries` gained `publish_job_id` (FK → `publish_jobs`, SET NULL) + `publish_attempted_at` + a `(status, date)` index (Neon DDL applied + round-trip verified). Publishing itself reuses `social-publishing.ts` untouched — no new Muapi surface. **+29 tests (369 total)**; `typecheck ✅ · tests ✅ · lint ✅ · build ✅`.

**Decisions (with rationale)** — all three gray areas went to the human before any code:
- **Auto-publish is an explicit per-item opt-in**, not automatic. `plan-phase-5.md` step 5 said publish any `generated` entry when due, which would mean approving a plan silently posts to real YouTube/TikTok/Instagram accounts days later. The cron now gates on `status = 'scheduled'`, which the user sets from the day panel. **Plan file amended in place** with the deviation. This is also what gives the documented `planned → generated → scheduled → published` flow meaning.
- **Cron trigger is a secret-protected route**, not a standalone `src/jobs/` script (which the plan's file list suggested). Any scheduler can drive it — a Replit Scheduled Deployment running curl is the deployment target — and unlike a script it is hand-testable, which is how the auth paths got verified this session.
- **A failed submit marks the entry `failed`**, it does not go back in the queue. Retrying a permanently broken entry every pass costs real money. Trade-off accepted: a transient Muapi 503 parks that entry. Re-scheduling a `failed` entry from the panel **is** the manual retry (opting in clears `publish_job_id`/`publish_attempted_at`, without which the claim could never match the row again).
- **Claim-before-submit on `publish_attempted_at`.** The one genuinely dangerous failure mode here is two overlapping runs each submitting the same due entry — two real posts, two charges. A conditional UPDATE makes the loser match no row. It is a column rather than a status flip because the status is already `scheduled` by that point.
- **Skips are non-destructive.** Not due / no connected account / unpublishable platform (e.g. `linkedin`) / no Asset Kit all leave the row untouched and *before* the claim, so a later run — or a later account connect — still picks it up.
- **Captions are capped in the scheduler**, not left to the param builders, which throw above their limits (150 TikTok / 2200 Instagram / 100 YouTube title) — a throw there would burn the entry to `failed`.
- **`CRON_SECRET` is optional in `src/env.ts`.** Making it required would stop the whole app booting where it isn't set; the route fails closed at request time instead.

**Verified**:
- **In-browser** (throwaway fixture harness at `/qa-day-panel`, since deleted — the real page is behind Clerk): all seven day-panel states — generated + connected → "Publish automatically"; scheduled + not in flight → "Cancel auto-publish"; scheduled + in flight → "Publishing to Instagram…" with no control; `failed` → "Publish automatically" (the retry); platform not connected → "Connect Instagram to publish this automatically."; `linkedin` and not-yet-generated → no control at all. Clicking the control unauthenticated gave a 401 → **error toast + optimistic status rolled back to "Ready"**.
- **Cron route against the live dev server**: no header → 401, wrong secret → 401, correct secret → 200 + summary JSON, GET and POST both. With no `CRON_SECRET` configured at all → 503.
- **Live scheduler probe against Neon** (`scripts/qa-scheduler-probe.mjs`, uncommitted): took a real calendar entry, made it due + `scheduled` with a real Asset Kit, ran the cron. The due query found it, the run reported `skipped: 1` (no connected account), and the row was left **unclaimed** — `publish_attempted_at` still null — exactly as designed. Row restored afterwards. Safe by construction: `social_accounts` is empty, so no publish could fire.

**Deferred (not silently dropped)**:
- **A real publish has never run through this path.** It needs a connected account and spends real money on a real public post — human-gated QA, same as every Phase-4 publish slice. The unproven leg is the submit call itself; everything either side of it is verified.
- **Per-entry retry/backoff** after a failed submit. Manual re-scheduling is the retry for now.
- **Flipping the Asset Kit's own `published` status** when an entry publishes, and the `planned` → `generated` transition on generation — both DEV-43.
- **No timezone handling**: entries are due at their stored `"HH:MM"` read as UTC. A user in Sydney scheduling "09:00" gets a 09:00 UTC post. The whole calendar is UTC (DEV-40/41), so this is consistent, not new — but it is now user-visible in a way it wasn't before.

**Tech debt observed**:
- **`CalendarEntry.platform` is free text but only three values can ever publish.** The panel, the scheduler, and `social-publishing.ts` each keep their own copy of the publishable-platform list (the schema's `SOCIAL_PLATFORMS` can't be value-imported into `src/lib` under the bare Node test runner — see DEV-15). Three copies is one too many.
- **No automated coverage for route handlers** anywhere in the repo, so `/api/cron/publish`'s auth logic is verified only by this session's curls. A route-level test setup would be worth having before more machine-called endpoints exist.
- `PROGRESS.md` "Current status" is now a single ~26 000-character line; it can't be read with a normal file read and has to be folded. Splitting it into bullets per phase is overdue.

**Committed** `ef1bbb8` on `main` + **pushed** to `origin/main` (fast-forward `2cc70ad..ef1bbb8`, human-requested same session). Linear DEV-42 left **In Progress** — no Needs Review status on this team, and the live-publish QA checklist is still open. Throwaway `artifacts/web/scripts/apply-calendar-scheduler-ddl.mjs` + `qa-scheduler-probe.mjs` left uncommitted per convention. A local-only `CRON_SECRET` was appended to `artifacts/web/.env.local` (gitignored) so the route could be exercised; **production/Replit needs its own value**.

### 2026-08-06 — DEV-70 investigated → NOT A BUG (browser-pane measurement artifact)

**Trigger**: human asked to proceed with DEV-70 (framer-motion appearing to strand elements at `opacity: 0`).

**Outcome: no app defect. DEV-70 closed as Canceled; the two prior "framer-motion bug" claims (DEV-41, DEV-69) were corrected in place above.**

**Root cause of the *observation***: the Browser pane runs the page with `document.visibilityState === "hidden"`, and in that state `requestAnimationFrame` never fires. framer-motion drives animations from rAF, so an element with `initial={{opacity: 0}}` never advances toward `animate` and sits invisible indefinitely. CSS animations/transitions are driven by the style/compositor system, not rAF, so they complete normally.

**Evidence (one page load, single measurement)**:
- `requestAnimationFrame` fired **0 times in 800 ms**; `document.hidden === true`.
- A CSS transition created in the same script reached `opacity: 1` normally.
- The **landing page** hero showed **15 elements stuck below full opacity** — the same freeze. This is the falsifying observation: the landing page working earlier had been the entire basis for the "server-first-paint / component-specific" hypothesis, and it fails identically once the pane is hidden. The variable was pane visibility all along, never SSR, the component, React 19, or the import path.

**Real users are unaffected** — rAF resumes when a tab becomes visible, so a background-tab load animates on focus.

**Actions taken**: closed DEV-70; corrected the misleading rationale comment in `calendar-view.tsx`; corrected the DEV-41 and DEV-69 log entries and the DEV-41 Linear completion comment. **Kept** the calendar's CSS `animate-in fade-in` — not as a bug workaround but on its own merits (no JS animation runtime for a simple fade; `/calendar` first-load JS 145 kB vs 185 kB). `PlanItemCard` keeps framer-motion, untouched.

**Lesson (the transferable bit)**: *verify the instrument before trusting the measurement.* Two "bugs" were filed off an environment artifact because the browser pane was treated as equivalent to a user's browser. Anything time- or frame-based — animations, transitions, timers, video playback, scroll/IntersectionObserver effects — is unreliable there. Check `document.hidden` and rAF liveness **before** concluding a rendering defect, and treat "it works elsewhere" as a hypothesis to test rather than a control.

### 2026-08-06 — Bugfix: DEV-69 product_showcase generation 422 (out-of-slice)

**Trigger**: human reported "the instagram generation on the content plan fails", with a screenshot of a plan at 6/7 complete, 1 failed. Interactive mode. Followed `WORKFLOW.md → Diagnose`.

**Feedback loop**: the failure reason *was* already persisted on the plan item (`generation-queue.ts → processItem`) but never displayed. Wrote a throwaway Neon probe (`scripts/qa-plan-failure-probe.mjs`, uncommitted per convention) to dump recent plans' items with their stored errors.

**Reproduced / diagnosed**:
- The failing item is **always `product_showcase`** — the only content type routed to the product-photo pipeline. Instagram `promo` / `testimonial` / `engagement` items in the *same* plans all completed, so the reported "Instagram" framing was a red herring.
- Stored error: `Muapi submit failed (422)` — `scene_description` and `image_url` both "Field required".
- Two compounding faults: (1) **wrong param names** — we sent `prompt`/`image`, the model requires `scene_description`/`image_url`, and `muapi.ts` serializes params straight into the body with no mapping layer; (2) **the required source image was never sent** — `ai-product-shot` is Image-to-Image (`model-router.ts` says so in a comment), yet the image was attached only `if (sourceImageUrl)` and `PhotoPipeline` had no such field, making it structurally impossible for the plan flow to supply one. `Product` is `{name, description?, price?}` — no image column exists anywhere.
- **Blast radius: 100%** of `product_showcase` items in every plan, not flaky. A 17 Jul plan generated one successfully, dating the regression.

**Fixed**:
- Queue falls back to the social-graphic pipeline when the matched product has no photo — reusing the fallback already there for "no products on the profile". This is what stops the failures today.
- Corrected the scene params to `scene_description` / `image_url`.
- `ProductPhotoService.generate` now fails loud without a source image instead of spending a call for an opaque 422.
- Added `imageUrl` to the queue's product shape and `sourceImageUrl` to `PhotoPipeline` — the seam for when product-image uploads exist.
- New pure `src/lib/plan-errors.ts` (`friendlyPlanItemError`) + surfaced on `PlanItemCard`. Raw error is provider output embedding status codes, model names and the **whole generated prompt**, so it is mapped to our own sentences and never interpolated; raw stays in DB + logs.

**Tests**: +9 (340 total). Regression coverage for the fallback, photo pass-through, both param names, fail-loud, and that no technical detail can leak to the card. Verified in-browser against a throwaway harness (since deleted) that all three failure shapes render and that `422` / "Muapi" / `scene_description` / prompt text never appear.

**Decisions**: chose the graphic fallback over repointing the model or wiring product-image uploads (human-confirmed). It's the smallest change that stops 100% failure, keeps the photo path correct for later, and needs no schema work.

**Known limitation (stated, not hidden)**: real product photography is now *unreachable* — nothing can attach a product image, so `product_showcase` renders as a graphic. Making it real needs an image field on profile products + upload UI. Logged on DEV-69.

**Tech debt observed**:
- ~~**DEV-70 filed** — framer-motion strands server-first-paint elements at `opacity: 0`~~ — **investigated and closed as NOT A BUG (2026-08-06).** Root cause was the measurement environment: the Browser pane runs `document.hidden === true`, `requestAnimationFrame` fires 0 times, and framer-motion is rAF-driven. Proven by measuring rAF directly (0 callbacks in 800 ms) while a CSS transition completed normally in the same page, and by reproducing the identical `opacity: 0` freeze on the **landing page** — the very page whose working animations had been the evidence for the "server-first-paint" hypothesis. Lesson recorded below.
- `muapi.ts` has **no param-mapping layer** — every caller must know each model's exact field names, and a mismatch surfaces only as a runtime 422 from the provider. A typed per-model param contract would have made this a compile error.
- Model contracts are unverified in tests: nothing catches "we send `prompt`, the model wants `scene_description`" short of a live call.

### 2026-08-06 — Phase 5: DEV-41 (STU-39) visual calendar UI — BUILT + browser-verified

**Phase**: 5 — Content Calendar + Scheduling (`plan-phase-5.md`). **Slice**: DEV-41 / STU-39, unblocked by DEV-40 (Done). Interactive mode, human present, explicit go-ahead ("go ahead, start DEV-41").

**Completed**: The `/calendar` page (DESIGN §9.8) — month grid (default) + week view, Monday-first, colour-coded by Content Family, legend, day slide-over with Asset Kit thumbnails and status badges, native HTML5 drag-to-reschedule with optimistic update + rollback, mobile list view (DESIGN §10), empty state, "Plan this week" CTA. Backed by a new pure `src/lib/calendar-view.ts` (all-UTC grid math), `CalendarService.rescheduleEntry` (owner-scoped), and `PATCH /api/calendar`. **+29 tests (331 total)**; `typecheck ✅ · tests ✅ · lint ✅ · build ✅`.

**Decisions (with rationale)**:
- **Zero new dependencies** (human-confirmed over date-fns / @dnd-kit / FullCalendar). The repo has **no React testing setup** — all tests are pure `src/lib/` logic — so keeping grid math pure means the slice is genuinely tested rather than smoke-tested, and drag needs no library.
- **Route `/calendar` in the `(dashboard)` group** (human-confirmed), matching DESIGN §9.8, `plan-phase-5.md`, and the group layout's own docstring. The sidebar's existing `/dashboard/calendar` link **was a 404** and was corrected.
- **All grid math UTC.** Entries persist at UTC midnight (DEV-40); building the grid in local time would shift entries a day for users behind UTC. Explicitly tested.
- **Content Family mirrors generation routing** (`product_showcase` → photo, `ugc_ad` → video, else graphic) rather than inventing a second taxonomy, since `content_type` holds 8 semantic values but DESIGN wants 3 colours.
- **Optimistic reschedule via an override map** layered over server props (not local state cloning), so `router.refresh()` reconciles naturally and a failed write rolls back — the UI never claims a write that didn't land.
- **`rescheduleEntry` is owner-scoped**: a cross-user drag is a "not found" (404), never a silent no-op the UI would render as success.

**Bugs found and fixed during in-browser verification** (a throwaway fixture harness, since deleted, since the real page is behind Clerk):
1. **Dots rendered colourless.** `tailwind.config.ts` scans `src/app`, `src/components`, `src/pages` — **not `src/lib`** — so the `bg-blue-500`/`bg-purple-500` strings originally written in `calendar-view.ts` were never generated. Fixed by keeping the *semantic* family in `src/lib` and moving *colours* to the component layer (`TypeDot`), plus a regression test asserting no class strings leak into the lib.
2. **Grid appeared stranded at `opacity: 0`.** ~~framer-motion bug~~ — **this diagnosis was wrong; corrected 2026-08-06 under DEV-70.** The Browser pane runs with `document.hidden === true`, where `requestAnimationFrame` never fires; framer-motion is rAF-driven, so its animations never advance and elements sit at their `initial` values. CSS animations are unaffected, which is why swapping to `animate-in fade-in` "fixed" it and masked the real cause. **No app bug — real users are unaffected** (rAF resumes when a tab becomes visible). The CSS fade was kept on its own merits: no JS animation runtime for a simple fade, and `/calendar` first-load JS is 145 kB instead of 185 kB.
3. **Dragged chip stranded semi-transparent.** Drag state was cleared only on `dragend`, which can never fire on the original node after a successful drop re-renders the chip into a different day cell. Now cleared on `drop` too.
4. **`/calendar` missing from the auth middleware** matcher while every other dashboard page was listed. Added (the page's own `auth()` check meant it was never exposed, but it should match its siblings).

**Verified in-browser**: month grid layout/padding/today marker, week view with times, day slide-over (labels, platforms, status tones, video icon), drag → move → **failed PATCH → rollback + error toast**, mobile list view at 375px, empty state.

**Deferred (not silently dropped)**:
- **"Plan Next Week" CTA** (DESIGN §9.8 lists it as secondary). `/plan` takes no week parameter and `PlanFlow` would ignore one, so a second button would visibly do the same thing as "Plan this week" — a UX lie. Shipped the AC-required "Plan this week" only. Needs week-scoped planning in `/plan` first.
- **Plan description on not-yet-generated items** in the slide-over (DESIGN §9.8). `calendar_entries` denormalizes title/platform/type but **not** description, so it needs a join back to the source Content Plan.
- **Keyboard-accessible reschedule.** Native HTML5 drag is pointer-only; entries remain viewable/openable by keyboard, but moving one requires a pointer.

**Tech debt observed**:
- ~~`framer-motion` can strand an element at `opacity: 0`~~ — **withdrawn 2026-08-06 (DEV-70): not a real defect**, an artifact of measuring in a hidden browser pane where `requestAnimationFrame` never fires. No action needed.
- **Four** separate content-type label maps now exist (`industry-templates.ts` `CONTENT_TYPE_LABELS`, `gallery-card.tsx`, `plan-item-card.tsx`, and gallery's own). This slice **reused** the lib-level one rather than adding a fifth; consolidating the other three is a candidate cleanup.
- `tailwind.config.ts` not scanning `src/lib` is a silent footgun — a lint rule or a comment in the config would prevent a repeat of bug 1.
- `pnpm lint` warns that `next lint` is deprecated (Next 16 removal); migration is a separate chore.

**Committed** `ad13bda` on `main` + **pushed** to `origin/main` (fast-forward `61f7c8a..ad13bda`, human-requested); DEV-41 → Done. Throwaway `artifacts/web/scripts/*.mjs` probes left uncommitted per convention. **Not done**: no live end-to-end run (real approve → entries → drag → DB) — behind Clerk sign-in, on the DEV-41 QA checklist.

### 2026-08-05 — UGC pipeline diagnosis (not a slice): "won't compile" → talking-head step failure, root-caused

- **Context**: after committing DEV-39, the human hit a failing UGC video build (wizard "We hit a snag" at "Creating your presenter video…") and said "UGC video will not compile." **Not a compile error** — every route serves 200; it's a runtime failure of the Phase-3 UGC pipeline's talking-head step (`ugc_lipsync` → `infinitetalk-image-to-video`). Diagnosed per WORKFLOW → Diagnose (feedback loop → reproduce → ranked hypotheses → instrument one variable). **This is Phase-3 debugging done mid-Phase-4 at the human's request; no slice, no Linear issue.**
- **Ground-truth error (from `ugc_jobs.steps` via a throwaway DB read):** `talking_head: failed — "Lip-sync portrait URL must be http(s): /presenters/grace-adeyemi.svg"`. Root cause: presenter `imageUrl` is a **relative-path SVG** (`presenters.ts`), and `buildLipsyncParams` → `requireHttpUrl` ([ugc-lipsync.ts:102](artifacts/web/src/lib/ugc-lipsync.ts:102)) rejects any non-http(s) URL. The known Phase-3 carryover (placeholder SVGs, not R2-hosted portraits) — **not** a DEV-39 regression.
- **Smoke-test (human chose "Option B" then "Option A", both temporary + reverted):** converted the 10 placeholder SVGs → 1024² PNG, uploaded to R2 (`presenters/*.png`, public + raster verified `HTTP 200 image/png`), repointed `imageUrl` → the R2 URLs. Retry then got a **new** error — `Muapi generation timed out after 60000ms (request_id …)` — proving **the plumbing works** (validation passed, Muapi fetched the R2 PNG, accepted the job, started generating). Bumped `MuapiService` `pollTimeoutMs` 60s→180s (singleton); retry still timed out at 180000ms. Polled the outstanding Muapi `request_id` **directly, out-of-band** → **`status: completed`**, real 2.2 MB MP4, `executionTime ≈260s (4.3 min)`, **cost $0.96 (76 credits)**. Sent the MP4 to the human.
- **Findings (the durable takeaways):**
  1. **Pipeline plumbing is proven end-to-end** — validate → R2 fetch → Muapi submit → **completed MP4**. Not the blocker.
  2. **⚠️ ARCHITECTURE (the real Phase-3 gap):** video steps take minutes each (infinitetalk **~260s** for a real ~15s VO; B-roll ~75s), but the UGC pipeline **polls inline inside one HTTP request** (`run()` executes all remaining steps synchronously). 60s default `pollTimeoutMs` is too short; even 180s isn't enough; and holding a request open across 4 video steps (~10min) is impossible on serverless. **The fix is background execution + client status-polling/webhooks, not a longer inline timeout** (the plan already noted "webhooks in production"). Candidate for a new Phase-3 slice.
  3. **⚠️ COST:** a full-length infinitetalk talking head billed **$0.96** — ~3.4× the DEV-29 probe's $0.28 and **over the plan's $0.80/video alert.** Budget ~$1/video for lip-sync alone; the "$0.20 catalog" figure badly understates real usage. (Logged in `.agents/memory/muapi-api-contract.md`.)
  4. **Portraits:** a faceless monogram **did render** (Muapi didn't reject it), so it's not blocking *a* render — but a usable talking head still needs real, rights-cleared portraits ([[presenter-portraits-phase3]]). Unchanged carryover.
- **Reverted** both temp changes (`muapi.ts` 180s bump, `presenters.ts` R2 repoint) — working tree clean, `git checkout` confirmed no diff. The 10 R2 test PNGs left in the bucket (harmless, now unreferenced). All throwaway scripts removed.
- **NOT committed** — these are investigation notes (this PROGRESS entry + the muapi memory addendum), no code change remains. **No Linear issue touched** (Phase-3, no active slice). Recommend filing a Phase-3 slice for the background-execution rework before the UGC pipeline can ship a real end-to-end video.

### 2026-07-28 — STU-31 (DEV-35): Social account connection flow — first Phase-4 slice (built + static-verified)

- **Context / deviation**: human said "start phase 4" while Phase 3 is **built but not yet verified** (3 carryovers open: DEV-33 PR merge, DEV-32 assembly probe re-run, real presenter portraits). Deviation logged here + on the Linear issue.
- **Contract de-risked first (the point of "probe first")**: Muapi's social-publishing API had never been exercised in this repo. `docs.muapi.ai` doesn't resolve and web search finds only *other* social APIs — but `muapi.ai/docs/social-publishing` documents it, and a live liveness probe (existing key, free connect-url generation) confirmed all 3 `POST /social/{youtube|tiktok|instagram}/connect-url` → **200** real OAuth URL, empty-body → **422**, `GET /social/ext/accounts` → **200 []**. Contract matches docs → built with confidence. **Key finding**: Muapi owns the OAuth callback itself and only bounces the user to our `redirect_to`, so we hold **no OAuth code and no tokens** — the callback is just a re-sync trigger.
- **Completed**: `social_accounts` table (Neon DDL + round-trip verified) + `src/lib/social-publishing.ts` (getConnectUrl/syncAccounts/listAccounts, injectable Muapi/db seams, 9 unit tests) + `POST /api/social/connect` + `GET /api/social/callback` (browser redirect target, webhook-class exception) + minimal `/social` page + `social-connections.tsx` (3 platform cards) + sidebar nav + middleware protection. `typecheck ✅ · tests 249/249 ✅ (+9) · lint ✅ · build ✅`.
- **Decisions**: (a) `platform` stored as string enum `youtube|tiktok|instagram`, **not** the plan's "1/2/3" — matches every schema enum + Muapi slugs (logged, CONTEXT.md **Platform ID** annotated). (b) lib module `import type`s from `@/db/schema` + keeps a local `PLATFORMS` runtime list — a runtime value-import from schema breaks the bare Node test runner (hit this live; the fix mirrors the codebase convention). (c) `redirect_to` derived from the request origin (no APP_URL env). (d) minimal UI — rename/disconnect is DEV-34; publish flows DEV-36/37/38; history DEV-39.
- **Verification**: unit tests cover platform resolution, connect-url building, the defensive Muapi→row mapping, and the sync upsert (skips disconnected/unmappable). Headless route surface: `/social` unauthed → clean sign-in redirect, `POST /api/social/connect` unauthed → 401 JSON, `GET /api/social/callback` unauthed → 307 to sign-in, no server errors, build compiles all 3 routes. **Live end-to-end OAuth is human-gated** — completing a real Google/TikTok/Instagram consent screen needs credentials I can't enter (QA checklist item).
- **Committed + Done**: at the human's explicit request, committed the slice on `main` as `5e4a028` (`phase-4: add social account connection flow (DEV-35)`) — staged the slice files only, left the 4 throwaway `scripts/*.mjs` probes uncommitted per convention. **Not pushed** (no push requested). DEV-35 → **Done** on the board.

- **Model — followed the plan, not the Linear title.** Title says `luma-flash-reframe`; the plan corrected it to `autocrop` (2026-07-10, human-approved — luma is $0.35, 7×). No re-litigation needed (correction predates this session + is documented). luma-flash-reframe left as the documented manual premium fallback (different schema, no segment window), un-wired.
- **Decision — new Asset Type, not a reuse.** The existing `reframe` route (`ideogram-v3-reframe`) is an *image* reframe consumed by DEV-19 product photos. `autocrop` is a video→video op, so it earns its own `video_reframe` entry (standard `autocrop` $0.05, no premium) — mirroring the DEV-32 `video_assemble` decision exactly. Existing image `reframe` route left untouched (no drive-by).
- **Decision — service shape.** A single-reframe primitive `reframeVideo({videoUrl, aspectRatio?, startTime?, endTime?, quality?})` + a `reframeToFormats` convenience that fans out (Promise.all) to the plan's default 9:16/1:1/16:9 set and returns the variants + summed cost, validating all formats/segment/URL *up front* so a bad request never spends. *Which* formats + R2 persistence into a multi-format Asset Kit = DEV-33's job (scope discipline, mirroring the DEV-32 "which clips is the orchestrator's job" note).
- **Live schema verified** (`GET /api/v1/models/autocrop`): `{ video_url, start_time (int req, def 0), end_time (int req, def 60), aspect_ratio? (enum 9:16/16:9/1:1/4:5/4:3/3:4, def 9:16) }` → `{ video }`→`outputs[0]`, $0.05 dynamic.
- **Completion probe** (`scripts/qa-reframe-probe.mjs`, kept uncommitted): real source MP4 → real 9:16 `video/mp4` (2.6 MB, Range-verified), ~32s inference, **$0.05 exactly** (== estimate). Success poll body is top-level `{status:"completed", outputs}` → parses cleanly through production `MuapiService`, so `videoUrl←generated.imageUrl` mapping is safe. First-try success (autocrop had capacity, unlike video-combiner's 503).
- **Tech debt observed (2, logged not fixed):**
  1. **`muapi.ts` failure-path parsing.** A *failed* Muapi job (seen on autocrop) wraps the record under `detail` (`{detail:{status:"failed", error}}`), so `MuapiService.generate`'s top-level `resultBody.status` read sees `undefined` → it polls until `pollTimeoutMs` and throws a generic "timed out" instead of surfacing the real provider error. General across all models (failure path only); success path is fine. Would improve error messages/retry decisions.
  2. **DEV-31 title vs model.** Linear DEV-31 title still says `luma-flash-reframe` — cosmetic drift vs the plan's `autocrop`; flagged in the completion comment for optional retitle.
- **Also fixed a probe bug** mid-session: initial `qa-reframe-probe.mjs` used autocrop's own schema-example source (`gtv-videos-bucket/…`), which **403s Muapi's downloader** → job failed on download; repointed to a Muapi CloudFront webasset (fetchable). Not a service bug — the pipeline feeds autocrop an R2/CDN master URL.
- **NOT committed / NOT pushed** — awaiting human review + go-ahead (interactive session). Issue left **In Progress** (Development team has no "Needs Review" status; completion comment signals review-ready, same pattern as DEV-32/STU-C4).
- **UI work**: no (pure `src/lib/` service + one routing entry; consumed by DEV-33 later).
